This option adds a visible frame around the tray opening. Keep the frame’s role explicit: presentation, locating a defined inner piece, or another proposed job. The frame alone is not evidence that the product is held securely.

### Separate frame geometry from the lid
PakFactory’s frame tray and lid reference identifies a double-wall frame tray paired with a double-wall lid. Its collapsible, prepared form is a catalog reference; your actual folds and prepared joints need a supplier drawing.
Show the tray outside envelope, frame opening and lid inside dimensions as separate records. Ask how each doubled wall is formed and retained. A top view of the frame doesn’t reveal the folded layers underneath.
Use the foldable gift box collection to compare a simpler tray when a frame has no defined purpose. This configuration earns a separate review because the opening, surrounding folds and matching lid create linked interfaces.

### Locate the product against the frame
Provide the complete product envelope, including any holder and the part visible above it. State whether the frame may touch the product or should surround another inner component. Mark sensitive surfaces as areas to discuss rather than assuming contact is acceptable.
For a hypothetical gift ornament, a holder could sit below a frame while the ornament is visible through its opening. Show the holder, frame and product in a side section. The visible opening and the product’s widest point may occur at different heights.
After forming the tray, add the holder and product without using the frame as a lever. Photograph their final relationship before placing the lid. That view can reveal a height issue that the closed set would hide.

### Use a paired-component review
Record both part revisions together. An approved tray paired later with another lid should reopen the fit check.

| Interface | Drawing input | Loaded observation
| Frame to product | Opening shape and visible height | Contact and access around the edge
| Double walls | Fold order and final layer position | Walls remain formed after loading
| Lid to tray | Inside lid and outside tray envelope | Contact at each side during placement
| Lid to contents | Highest packed point | Clearance when fully closed
Keep contact with the tray perimeter distinct from contact with the product. Increasing the lid cavity may answer one issue while leaving the other unresolved.

### Confirm supplied state and sample scope
Ask how tray and lid arrive, which seams are prepared and who forms the double walls. Include separate packing for the two components if that is proposed. Counts and pairing should be clear before a quotation is compared.
Review the proposed material and caliper with the actual fold sequence. Close the loaded set, lift the lid and remove the product. Inspect the frame and walls afterwards, naming any part that has moved.
These are configuration observations. They don’t establish a protective capacity, material certification or a distribution result. Any handling assessment should use the complete proposed system and an agreed method.
If the frame is intended to conceal the holder edge, show the desired visible boundary on the opening view. Check it before lid placement and again after lid removal. A holder that shifts during closure may reveal a different area afterwards. Name the actual observation without describing the frame as a retaining feature unless that construction is confirmed.

### Send the paired set, not two unrelated sizes

The frame tray and enclosing sleeve are separate components with a moving interface. Their material, fold and seam details belong on the same sample record. A closed beauty view conceals most of that interface.

### Draw the frame perimeter and sleeve interior
The frame tray and sleeve reference identifies a double-wall frame tray paired with a sleeve. Ask for the proposed drawing to show which perimeter parts travel nearest the sleeve walls and where its seam lies.

### Run entry and removal with the frame loaded
Form the double walls and frame as proposed, then load the actual holder and product. Insert the tray along the intended direction. Pause at entry, midway and the final closed position.

### Use a travel contact map
Assign contact locations on a simple perimeter sketch. Each observation should include the travel stage and which component touches.

| Travel stage | Area to inspect | Record
| Entry | Leading frame edge and sleeve mouth | Initial alignment and first contact
| Midway | Frame sides and internal seam | Location of rubbing or obstruction
| Closed | Loaded height and end position | Seated envelope and available grip
| Removal | Trailing edge and product height | Exit contact and customer action
A contact at the mouth and a contact along a seam are different issues. Send separate marked photographs so the supplier can compare them with the construction drawing.

### Agree supplied preparation and the opening end
State how tray and sleeve arrive and who forms each. Request a view of prepared double-wall folds and the sleeve seam. Confirm whether either end is closed and how the customer begins removal.

Inspect the frame and sleeve surfaces after the agreed actions. Record visible contact marks as sample observations. This method doesn’t quantify wear life, friction or protective performance; any required evaluation needs its own scope.
Mark which frame edge leads during entry. A tray may look symmetrical from above while its folds or product arrangement differ at the two ends. Reversing it during a trial changes the travel map. If both directions are permitted, keep two records with their respective leading edges and compare the same stages.

### Plan your packaging requirements

#### When to consider a drawer box
A sliding tray may be worth exploring when the opening sequence and access to the jewelry are part of the presentation. Review the complete jewelry set inside the proposed tray, including clasps, posts, chains and any separate card. Check the sleeve-to-tray movement, pull attachment and removal space on an appropriate sample.

#### Prepare the quotation
Share the preferred structure, approximate dimensions, jewelry and insert layout, artwork and finish preferences, expected quantity for each design, destination country and target timing. Agree which costs the quotation covers: box, insert, decoration, setup or tooling where applicable, samples, packing and delivery. Confirm currency, quantity basis, trade terms, destination and validity before comparing offers.

#### Define the sample review

A rosette-top carton description identifies a sequentially folded petal closure, separate tuck bottom and flat delivery with a prepared side seam. Those are narrow construction examples, not universal dimensions or Packrivo manufacturing claims.
Ask the converter to label every petal, its crease and the edge it overlaps. Mark which petal moves first and where the final one enters the offered overlap. Keep the actual panel count on the drawing rather than inferring it from the style name.
Supply the complete product and holder envelope. Identify the highest local projection beneath the moving top. A gift can sit inside the carton yet approach a petal as that panel crosses the opening. Record that movement with the product state and offered drawing.

### Separate bottom setup from petal closing
Request the actual base construction and delivered preparation. State who forms the bottom before loading, which joints arrive prepared and what remains for local packing. The petal-top choice does not settle the bottom operation.
Compare the paperboard roll-end dust-flap carton directly. That option forms rolled walls and closes dust flaps with a top tuck. This flower-petal option needs a sequence of overlapping top panels and a final petal engagement, plus a distinct release touchpoint.
Form the offered base, load the complete contents and follow the named petal sequence. Pause before the last petal enters and inspect the top-panel/product relationship. Then open through the intended touchpoint and record which petal releases first. Ask for a located revision if the agreed route needs a different action.

### Use a petal sequence and height record
The following hypothetical four-petal proposal is an original brief example. The offered design can have another panel count or arrangement.

| Closure field | Illustrative input | Review evidence
| Order P1→P2→P3 | Named moving panels on drawing FT1 | Partly closed views in the offered sequence
| Final petal P4 | Entry beneath the offered receiving overlap | Last-entry detail with complete contents
| Product envelope | Holder and care card in final positions | Highest projection below moving petals
| Opening touchpoint | Edge used to begin customer release | Loaded reopening route
| Bottom B1 | Offered tuck or other proposed base | Supplied-state and local setup record

### Handoff the actual closing route
The converter supplies the panel, overlap and base drawings. The packing owner confirms setup and closing actions with the actual contents. The buyer chooses the intended opening route or requests another sample tied to a particular petal or height question.
Use the tuck-end carton option for a simpler end-closure comparison and the supplied-state record for preparation responsibility. Send the petal sequence, contents height and opening question through Get a Quote. MOQ is 500 pieces per design; materials, samples and commercial terms are confirmed for the project.

A foldable lid-and-base box uses two paperboard components that are formed separately before the lid covers the loaded base. ### Treat the base and lid as separate constructions

A two-piece appearance does not tell you which panels fold, lock or arrive pre-glued. Ask for the proposed base drawing and lid drawing, with the remaining local work marked on each. Identify any doubled walls, corner tabs, adhesive areas or additional parts before assigning packing responsibilities.

Measure the formed base for its usable product space, then discuss the lid overlap against the actual assembled construction. The outside dimensions of a flat blank answer a different question. Added wrapping, a holder or a card that rises above the walls can change the finished lid decision.

Decide where the formed components are stored before loading. A packing team might form both pieces in advance or assemble one complete gift at a time. Those are different workflows, with different staging questions. Record the preferred process and ask whether the supplied packing arrangement supports it.

### Choose the lift-off format with its local work

Compare this option with the rigid lid-and-base format when deciding whether local forming work belongs in the project. Both have a separate cover, but their component construction and delivery state need individual confirmation. Compare a one-piece folding carton when an attached closing flap better suits the loading process.

For an illustrative gift kit, a pouch and a care card may need a shared holder inside the base. A lid that fits an empty box may respond differently to that complete layout. Specify the removal sequence and the part the customer should grasp after the cover is lifted.

### Describe the two-piece packing job

| Field | Record for review

| Base | Usable formed space, wall build, locks and support for the product.

| Lid | Formed interior, overlap depth and the intended lift-off action.

| Components | Holder, tissue, cards and their loaded height.

| Assembly | Folds, consumables, instructions and the person completing each step.

| Delivery | State of both pieces, bundles and proposed carton packing.

| Artwork | Named base and lid files, print faces and finish areas.

Keep the top artwork orientation related to how the base is loaded. If a logo should face the customer at a particular opening angle, show that view in the brief. The flat and assembled scope worksheet lets the proposals identify what arrives and what remains to do.

### Review one complete gift

- Form the base and lid using the supplied instructions.
- Inspect corners, locks, seams and the intended wall positions.
- Load all items and extras into their proposed places.
- Fit the lid, then lift it using the intended customer grip.
- Remove the contents and inspect contact with the walls and holder.
- Check artwork orientation and fold appearance on the formed pieces.

Record a base or lid correction against the appropriate drawing. If a change affects overlap or loaded height, identify the paired component that must be checked again. Approving one blank should not silently approve a different assembled pair.

## Custom Folded Box Liner Sourcing
A folded box liner is an internal component formed against the walls or selected surfaces of a box. Consider this option when the brief needs to describe where the product or its retail pack meets the surrounding enclosure. Plan the liner's coverage, folds and loading sequence first, then decide whether a separate holder is needed to locate the contents.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Define liner wall contact and corner folding with the finished interior.

### Product specifications
Minimum orderMOQ 500 pieces per design.
Get a QuoteShare your dimensions, quantity and delivery destination.
The minimum is Packrivo’s project threshold. Availability for the chosen structure, material and finish is checked for your project.

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Give the liner a defined area

Show which surfaces the proposed liner covers: side walls, a base, selected corners or another stated area. Identify the interfaces where it meets the outer box or another component. A liner may reduce the usable space, so its formed condition belongs in the interior measurement discussion.

Ask for the unfolded and formed views together. Fold tabs, seams and overlaps can change the local surface and the space available to load the product. Describe how the liner is retained, including whether the proposed construction simply rests in the enclosure or uses a specific attachment.

Mark the liner’s starting orientation in the loading instructions. A rotated part can put a seam against a different product surface.

Keep the intended contact conditions visible. The product might have a sensitive finish, a projecting part or an existing retail cover. Name the surface that may touch the liner and the material information the supplier needs to address. The word “paper” does not settle contact suitability or a product-specific protection requirement.

### Separate lining from locating

A paperboard holder can create cutouts or supports to position the contents. A liner introduces a different task: covering a defined area around them. An internal divider separates multiple items. Choose by the required contact and movement, and combine components only when their jobs are clear.

For an illustrative candle carton, a wall liner could be considered alongside a base support. The support determines how the vessel sits; the liner affects its relationship to the enclosure. Review their combined thickness, contact and removal rather than accepting them as two unrelated accessories.

### Prepare a coverage and loading record

| Field | Describe the liner in use

| Outer box | Current construction, usable interior and closure.

| Coverage | Walls or surfaces lined, open areas and the purpose of each covered area.

| Construction | Material proposal, folds, seams, overlaps and retention method.

| Product | Geometry, finish sensitivities and named contact surfaces.

| Operations | Liner assembly, product loading and customer removal sequence.

| Project | Component quantities, destination, drawing versions and sample scope.

Keep artwork instructions tied to the surfaces that remain visible after forming. If the liner is printed, identify which folds and overlaps cover part of the graphic. A layout that looks correct while flat can present a different image inside the loaded carton.

### Review the lined enclosure as one configuration

- Form the liner and place it in the proposed outer box.
- Inspect folds, retention and all changes to the usable interior.
- Load the actual product and any separate supports.
- Close the pack and inspect contact or interference with the lid.
- Open the pack and remove the product by hand.
- Record the contact observations and agreed next review.

If the liner shifts, bunches or prevents closure, identify the affected fold, attachment or measurement. The sample revision record keeps that issue linked to the box and liner versions. A later holder change can reopen the same contact question.

### Agree component scope and quantity

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. List the liner, enclosure and any holder separately, with their quantities and sample tasks. Final material, construction, pricing, production conditions and delivery timing are reviewed for the project.

The original box-liner structural reference distinguishes a wall-lining component from cavity-level holders. Its specifications and performance descriptions remain specific to that source.

### Separate the two pieces and their jobs

The tray holds the contents and provides the surface from which they are removed. The sleeve surrounds that tray and establishes its opening path. Each piece has its own fold pattern, seam and artwork surfaces. Ask for the proposed construction of both, including whether the tray uses locking corners, glued parts or an additional holder.

Describe the loaded arrangement before discussing sleeve dimensions. Jewelry cards, small accessories and folded tissue can sit above the tray edge or catch at its entrance. Name the highest item and the surfaces that may touch the sleeve. The useful check is the assembled and loaded combination, rather than the tray and sleeve measured independently.

Choose how the user starts the movement: a thumb opening, a pull detail or access to an exposed tray edge. Indicate whether the tray should slide out fully. A stop, if proposed, needs its own explanation and review; it should not be inferred from a rendered picture.

### Compare folded and rigid drawer construction

A rigid drawer jewelry box and a folding tray-and-sleeve option may share an opening idea but have different component construction and preparation tasks. Compare the actual supplied state, corner formation, tray wall build and remaining local work. Those are more useful distinctions than a blanket claim that one version is cheaper or stronger.

For an illustrative gift with a necklace card and a separate pouch, the packing team must know whether the card loads before the tray is closed and whether the pouch stays below its rim. That sequence can change both the holder and the sleeve clearance. Capture the sequence while the layout is still being discussed.

### State what arrives at the packing bench

| Brief field | Record before comparing proposals

| Tray | Formed usable space, wall construction, corner locks and loading order.

| Sleeve | Inner opening, seam position, print faces and starting grip.

| Supply state | Flat, partly formed or assembled for each component.

| Assembly | Who folds, locks, glues, loads and checks the complete pack.

| Additional parts | Holder, card, pouch or tissue with separate quantities.

| Project | Design quantities, destination, artwork versions and open questions.

Use the flat and assembled delivery worksheet to compare the same completed pack. Include local consumables and storage requirements in the questions. A flat drawing describes the proposed construction; it does not supply a measured assembly time or freight saving.

### Check folds, loading and travel together

- Assemble the supplied pieces using the proposed instructions.
- Check that corners and seams reach their intended positions.
- Load all contents, including the holder and printed extras.
- Slide the loaded tray, checking contact at the entrance and along the travel.
- Remove the gift by hand and inspect the fold and contact areas.
- Record which drawing and sample versions were reviewed.

If a change is needed, identify whether it belongs to the tray, sleeve or packed contents. Widening the sleeve might alter the closed appearance; changing the tray might affect its holder. Keep those linked consequences visible before accepting the next revision.

### Prepare a project-specific proposal

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. Ask for the tray, sleeve, holder, print work and sampling to be described as separate scope items. Final material construction, pricing, production conditions and delivery timing are agreed for the reviewed configuration.

### Confirm the corners before drawing the display front
PakFactory’s four corner display reference describes a collapsible display with four pre-glued corners. Use that detail as a structural reference, then request the actual corner and front-cutout drawing for your project.

| Display state | Unit to remove | Observation to capture

| Last unit | Final intended position | Visibility and grip without tipping tray
| Refill | Replacement units | Entry route and final facing position

A center opening photo can miss a corner unit behind a taller section. Keep that extra position in the depletion record if it belongs to the intended retail arrangement.

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

This option focuses on a collapsible base with a hinged lid and four prepared corners. It should be compared through setup and closure actions. The number of corners doesn’t establish a performance ranking.

### Identify prepared corners and the lid fold
The four corner box reference describes a collapsible hinged-lid structure with four pre-glued corners. Request the supplier’s proposal to identify those joints and the lid’s actual edge details.
Keep the base joints separate from the lid hinge on the drawing. Mark the position the base should reach after forming. Ask whether any lid-side detail requires a manual tuck or other action beyond lifting the base walls.
A six corner pre glued carton adds another prepared-corner configuration to compare. Make that comparison with supplied and formed views, not an assumption that one style is automatically stronger.

### Form the empty base before checking the product
Start with the sample in the agreed delivered state. Follow the proposed forming instruction and photograph each base corner afterwards. Note any corner that remains partly folded or needs a separate movement.
For a hypothetical small gift set, form the tray first, then place a card holder and accessory inside. This sequence helps show whether the holder interferes with a corner or whether the corner itself was incomplete before loading.
Raise and lower the attached lid while the base remains empty. Then repeat with the complete contents. Record the difference if the lid changes position when a product, tissue fold or card is added.

### Keep a base-and-lid comparison record
Use one sample revision for all rows. Include photographs from the supplied and final loaded states.

| Part | Empty-state check | Loaded-state check
| Four base joints | Each reaches its intended formed position | Holder or product leaves that position unchanged
| Lid hinge | Movement and resting position | Clearance above the complete contents
| Lid edges | Proposed closing action | Contact with walls, card or wrap
| Customer access | Starting grip location | Product removal after lid opens
If a lid edge catches, name the contacting part and the stage. A request to add depth may solve a product-height issue but won’t necessarily resolve a base corner formed incorrectly.

### Agree the material and packing handoff
State the board grade, caliper, print surfaces and prepared joints proposed for the final sample. If those details remain open, leave them as explicit questions. A material substituted for an early mock-up should be named in its record.
The delivery-state guide helps define who forms the carton and adds the contents. Keep separate components visible in that scope rather than treating the folded box as a complete gift set.
Review corner formation, closure and removal as observations. Agree any weight, handling or distribution requirement separately; a clean lid fit doesn’t demonstrate protection. Final outside dimensions should be checked in the loaded closed state if that is the specified reference condition.
Keep lid-edge formation in the setup instruction if the supplier proposal includes it. The phrase ‘four prepared corners’ should not conceal a separate lid action. A reviewer should be able to identify the finished base and the ready-to-close lid as two checkpoints, even when both belong to the same continuous blank.

### Submit the joint and lid record

### Artwork and presentation choices
Decide which artwork the buyer sees before opening and which appears inside the mailer. Mark folds and sealing areas on the same drawing used for the loaded-pack review.

### Options to compare

- Try a bold exterior pattern, high-contrast band or full-color interior print.

- Keep graphic elements clear of folds and adhesive areas.

- Separate doorstep presentation from shipping test requirements.

### Compare weight, delivery and printing against one packed system

The front-tuck corrugated mailer shown on this page is one structural option. A request for a lighter box, flat delivery or printed surfaces still needs an identified closure and complete contents; those attributes do not define a new structure by themselves.

| Option to compare | Keep in the same brief | Question to resolve

| Lower proposed box weight | Complete product and retail pack, accessories, wrap or insert, loaded dimensions, destination and handling route. | What construction and sample review support this proposal for the same contents? Lower box weight alone does not establish protection.

| Flat delivery | Delivered parts and outline, formed interior, closure, insert installation and any fastening components. | Who forms the box, fits the insert, loads it and closes it, and what instructions arrive with the parts?

| Printed mailer | Current drawing and artwork, inside and outside panels, opening orientation and the physical color reference. | Which print areas cross folds, meet seals or sit beneath a shipping label, and what remains visible after packing?

Request each offered closure by name and drawing. Do not label an alternative front-tuck merely because it shares the same weight or printed appearance. Keep the mailer and fitted insert together in the loaded review, while retaining any distribution-performance evaluation as its own scope.

Compare flat and assembled offers against the same finished arrangement, with remaining local work identified. Review printed panels on the formed candidate rather than treating a flat screen preview as physical color or finished-pack evidence.

### Project details to agree

Final materials, construction, price, sample scope and delivery timing are confirmed for the agreed project with the relevant supplier.

For a corrugated mailer brief with a front-tuck opening. Board, fit, packed weight and the shipping route must be reviewed together; the rendered box does not establish a tested transit result.

A full-flap auto-bottom carton needs two base records: how the prepared bottom forms, and where the coverage flap sits beneath the product. #### Structure

### Locate the coverage flap inside the formed carton
The full-flap auto-bottom reference names this construction. Esko also illustrates full-flap auto-bottom carton configurations in its ArtiosCAD style catalogue. Use those references to identify the idea, then obtain the actual blank offered for your gift. The position and shape of its coverage flap must be visible on that drawing.
Ask for an inside base view and an underside view. Mark the coverage flap, its fold, the underlying prepared joints and any exposed overlap. Those views answer different questions: the underside explains erection, while the inside shows what the loaded product touches.
Measure the product footprint in its finished primary packaging. Include a projecting candle-jar rim, a jewelry backing card or any separate accessory. ### Separate erection from product contact

Record whether a flap edge catches the contents, whether a corner rests on an overlap and whether an insert changes the contact location. Leave any load or distribution evaluation as a separately agreed task.

### Use a coverage and contact record
The following entries are a hypothetical candle-gift planning example. Replace them with the proposed drawing and observations; they are not completed sample results.

| Base interface | Example brief entry | Evidence to request
| Coverage flap B1 | Show B1 beneath the jar and identify its fold | Offered blank plus inside base view
| Prepared joint J1 | Identify factory preparation separately from B1 | Underside view and supplied state
| Jar footprint | Finished jar is the direct contact item | Product dimensions and contact map
| Accessory insert | Card holder sits beside the jar | Loaded interior photograph
| Overlap question | Keep any catching edge open for review | Named observation and proposed revision

### Give the next question to the right owner
The converter should supply the flap and joint drawing. Your packing owner should confirm the erection and loading order, including whether the carton arrives prepared or needs an additional action. The buyer then decides which observed contacts need a revision or a further sample. A single closed-carton photograph cannot replace these three records.
Use the folding carton option to compare the broader box choice, and the flat and assembled delivery method to record preparation responsibility.

The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

This option is distinct from a carton that closes by tucking a flap into the body. Its brief needs both geometry and production handoff. An assembled concept can hide the operation that your packing bench would have to complete.

### Show coverage at each end
The full overlap seal end reference describes fuller end flaps and warns that large-volume assembly requires glue equipment. Use those statements to ask process questions, not to assume a particular supplier machine or your own production capability.

### Assign the sealing work before asking for samples

A hypothetical accessory bundle might be inserted through one open end after the other has been prepared. The operator then closes the remaining flaps and completes the agreed seal. | Stage | Geometry required in the proposal | Responsible operation
| First end | Flap order and coverage | Who prepares and seals it
| Loading | Open-end envelope and product orientation | Who inserts the complete contents
| Second end | Last flap and sealing surfaces | Method, equipment and operator
| Customer opening | Intended access route | Instruction and acceptable opened condition
The record makes a missing process visible before price comparison. A quotation for empty flat cartons can’t be treated as a quotation for packed, sealed units.

### Inspect the loaded seam and opening
Confirm the supplied state, material and caliper. View the end seam after the proposed operation with complete contents present. Photograph any flap lift, uneven placement or visible contact with the product.
Open the sample using the intended customer route. Record where opening begins and how the product is removed. If the pack is intended for a single opening, say so in the brief; if reuse is desired, ask whether another closure option is more appropriate.
These observations don’t demonstrate seal integrity, tamper evidence or transit protection. Any such requirement needs a defined evaluation and responsible evidence. Keep vendor promotional protection language out of the approval record.
Record the quantity being discussed for the sealing operation as well as the carton design quantity. A small appearance sample may be assembled under different conditions from a production run. Ask the supplier to state which conditions its sample represents, then keep that explanation with the seam photographs before using them as an approval reference.

#### Structure

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Follow the structure through the top

The raised top changes the carton silhouette and the space available for loading and closing. Ask which panels create the handle, how those panels join and which tabs or folds hold the top in position. A drawing should show the opening before loading and the closed carrying state.

Keep the usable product cavity separate from the raised top. A vessel or accessory should be described in the position it occupies inside the main body, including its holder and any card. Then identify whether a projecting part comes near the sloping top panels or the handle attachment.

Describe the person’s carrying action. The opening needs to suit the intended grip, while the assembled package has to be assessed for its actual weight and handling conditions. Ask the responsible supplier to propose the construction and relevant review. Avoid selecting a material or accepting a carrying claim from the handle’s appearance alone.

### Compare carrying and presentation tasks

A standard folding carton can place the emphasis on loading and end closure. A gable format brings the carry feature into the carton itself. A separate gift bag and tissue set creates a different component list and handoff sequence. Compare the complete job rather than treating the bag or handle as interchangeable decoration.

For an illustrative candle gift, the jar, insert and information card need to be inside the layout used for review. The top panels might need space to fold around that arrangement. The carry action also depends on the base and the insert, so the handle review should refer to the same complete configuration.

### Prepare the carry and loading brief

| Brief field | Specify or identify as open

| Product layout | Complete contents, weight, display orientation and projecting parts.

| Body | Usable interior, loading opening, base and holder arrangement.

| Raised top | Panel construction, closing sequence and space above the product.

| Carry feature | Grip opening, attachment, intended handling and review requirement.

| Presentation | Print orientation on the body and top, including folded areas.

| Scope | Quantity by design, supplied state, assembly responsibility and destination.

The measurement method worksheet helps separate the body cavity, overall height and grip opening. Name their locations so a quote or sample drawing does not silently substitute one measurement for another.

### Review the complete carry sequence

- Form the base and top using the proposed instructions.
- Load the product, holder and printed extras in the intended order.
- Close the top and inspect tabs, folds and any attachment points.
- Review the agreed carry action with the actual loaded configuration.
- Open the package and remove the product using the intended route.
- Inspect the handle, base and contact areas after that review.

Record an issue at its location: a top panel catching a card, an uncomfortable grip or a tab that does not reach its intended position. A change to the handle can affect the top artwork and the supplied flat layout, so keep the structural and print versions paired.

### Agree the project conditions

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. Separate the carton, holder and additional components in the proposal. Final construction, material, pricing, production conditions and delivery timing are agreed against the reviewed brief.

The original gable-box structural reference provides a named example of the raised-top format. A project-specific carrying review is still needed for the proposed contents and construction.

Use this option for a flat product or document-style bundle whose thickness is part of the purchase brief. Confirm the actual folder construction, lock and delivered form on the supplier proposal. It isn’t a general claim that one size will fit every bundle.

### Describe the contents as a stack
PakFactory’s gusset folder reference identifies expanding sides for flat contents. Use that relationship to describe the side-fold job. The expansion you can accept for your own pack requires a drawing and loaded sample.
Measure the entire stack in its packed condition, including backing board, wrap and any raised component. Record the stack’s largest thickness and where that point occurs. A folded booklet and a flat card with a small attached item don’t create the same envelope.
For a hypothetical gift stationery set, the basic bundle might contain a few cards, while the larger bundle adds envelopes and a care sheet. Those are two named configurations, not two estimates of the same product.

### Review the side folds at each thickness
Load the thinner bundle first and place the folder in the intended closed orientation. Observe the gussets from the side. Note loose areas, panel bowing or contents that sit at a different depth than the drawing suggests.
Use a fresh comparison sample for the thicker bundle if repeated folding has changed the first. Inspect the same locations, then open the lock and remove the contents. Include the customer’s likely grip rather than lifting the stack out through an unplanned gap.
Compare other folding formats if the product needs a fixed-depth cavity instead. This option’s core decision is how a defined thickness range meets side-fold geometry and the lock.

### Keep a two-configuration comparison
Write actual measurements in the blank parts of your own record. The table identifies the fields; it doesn’t provide universal allowances.

| Record | Thinner bundle | Thicker bundle
| Contents | List each included piece | List additions and changed parts
| Thickness | Measure packed high point | Measure packed high point
| Gusset position | Photograph both side folds | Repeat the same views
| Lock and access | Record closure and removal action | Record contact or obstruction
A thicker pack may need a different structure rather than more force at the lock. Send the observation to the supplier with the drawing revision and ask which part needs changing.

### Confirm preparation and material together
Ask how the folder arrives and whether the side folds or joining areas are already prepared. State who forms it, inserts the stack and closes the lock. A packing instruction should show the side folds before the cover is pushed down.
Review the proposed board and caliper against the actual fold pattern. If artwork spans a gusset, view it loaded at both thicknesses before confirming its position. A flat print proof alone can’t show which part of that artwork becomes hidden in a fold.
Keep the sample checks on fit, formation, opening and presentation. Don’t treat them as measured protection or durability. Any handling evaluation should name the conditions, contents and agreed acceptance rule separately.
A bundle with one local raised part needs a side sketch as well as its maximum thickness. Show whether that part sits beside a gusset or under the cover. Two stacks with the same maximum thickness can place their high points differently. Keep that position fixed in the thin-and-thick comparison instead of treating one number as the complete contents description.

### Send the thickness range, not a vague size
Use Get a Quote to send both contents lists, measured stacks, gusset observations, quantity and destination. The minimum is 500 pieces per design. The proposed folder, material, lock, samples and commercial terms are confirmed against that brief.

## Custom Hanging Tab Retail Carton Sourcing
A hanging-tab carton adds a display panel or attachment above or beside a folding box so the pack can be presented on a retail fixture. Consider this option when the product should remain inside a carton while the retailer hangs the pack. Brief the fixture interface, assembled weight and product position together, then review the complete package in the intended display arrangement.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Identify the actual hang slot and complete pack before the fixture review.

### What affects the price

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials

### Compare an integral five-panel hanger with an attached hanging feature

In a five-panel hanger proposal, identify the projecting hanger panel separately from the carton body and its end closures. Show its fold, hole and printed face on the actual drawing. A projecting hanger should not be mistaken for the flap used to load or close the box.

| Hanging feature | Construction to identify | Assembly question

| Integral projecting panel | Panel outline, fold, hang opening and relationship to the carton closure. | Does the panel remain raised or fold at another stage, and which end receives the product?

| Separately attached feature | Additional part, attachment position, joining proposal and receiving carton surface. | What arrives attached, what remains to join and who performs that work?

Provide the real hook or fixture, surrounding display space and complete packed weight. Review three actions with the same loaded sample: resting on the fixture, lifting the pack off it and opening the contents. Record a problem against the action and interface where it occurs.

Keep a fixture observation tied to its sample and load. Neither the structure name nor a catalog picture establishes a hanging capacity for a different product or material.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Describe the fixture before the tab

Record the retailer’s hook or fixture details and the space around the display. A generic hanging hole does not define its shape, position or clearance. Show where the loaded carton should sit, which face the customer sees and how the carton is taken off the fixture.

Ask whether the proposed hanging feature is part of the paperboard blank or a separately attached component. That changes the material list, assembly work and review of the attachment. Identify any fold between the display panel and the box body so the intended hanging position is clear in the drawing.

Keep the hanging location related to the complete product layout. Jewelry cards, pouches or other holders may put the contents to one side. Record the loaded weight and orientation rather than assuming the front artwork and the actual displayed position will align.

### Keep retail access separate from loading

The box still needs a defined loading and opening route. An operator may load through one end while a customer opens another. A hanging panel near that opening could affect either action. Show the top or end closure and the tab on the same proposed construction.

Compare a tuck-end carton when a shelf position is sufficient. The hanging option adds a fixture and display review to that task. For an accessory displayed openly on a card, a different mounting arrangement may be useful; a carton is a separate choice about enclosure and information space.

### Prepare the display specification

| Input | What the supplier needs

| Fixture | Hook or fixture geometry, available space and removal movement.

| Hanging feature | Integral or attached, position, hole shape and proposed construction.

| Packed contents | Weight, layout, holder, projections and orientation.

| Carton access | Loading end, retail opening, closure and remaining assembly.

| Artwork | Visible faces, fold zones, required copy and barcode position.

| Project | Design quantities, destination, display conditions and sample scope.

Use a simple layout drawing to connect the hole, box and product. If the retailer provides a fixture specification, keep its name or revision with the packaging files. The artwork handoff checklist helps retain that relationship as the panels and print files are prepared.

### Review the loaded display and customer action

- Assemble and load the proposed carton with every included component.
- Place it on the intended fixture and inspect its resting position.
- Check hole access, tab folds and any attachment interface.
- Read the visible information from the expected customer view.
- Remove the pack, open it and take out the product.
- Record the agreed display review and any change required.

For an illustrative earrings-and-pouch pack, the hanger position and the card layout could both affect how it rests. If the pack tilts, the next discussion should identify the layout or hanging interface to review. Increasing a hole or changing the tab alone might not address the complete arrangement.

### Plan one reviewable retail pack

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. Identify the carton, hanging component if separate, holder and sampling work in the proposal. Material, production conditions, pricing and delivery timing are confirmed for the project.

The original hanging and locking-tab reference illustrates a carton with a hanging feature. It supports the construction question, without establishing a load rating for this option.

The angled shape is a structural choice, not only a visual theme. It changes where a product or holder meets the walls. Keep those contacts visible in the inquiry even if the outside artwork is still undecided.

### Identify six walls in the formed position
The PakFactory hexagon catalogue confirms the named packaging option. Ask for a proposed dieline and assembled drawing; its title alone doesn’t establish your closure geometry, dimensions or contents arrangement.

### Make a cavity worksheet
Use the table for a specific drawing revision. Each row asks for a view or measurement that a general box-size field can miss.

| Area | Input to provide | Check on the formed sample
| Wall ring | Six-wall plan and front face | Wall sequence and seam position
| Product outline | Oriented packed envelope | Closest wall and corner contacts
| Bottom | Closure drawing and forming order | Base complete before loading
| Top | Opening direction and grip | Closure clears product and added card
The review should distinguish a round product that rotates from a holder that fixes one orientation. Choose which behavior you want; don’t assume the shape settles that question.

### Review forming, loading and removal in order
Confirm what arrives prepared, including the side seam and any bottom work. Form all six walls and the base before placing the holder. Photograph the empty cavity so a later fit issue can be compared with its unloaded geometry.
Load the complete product set, close the top and then open it as a customer would. Check whether fingers can reach the item without levering against a wall. Photograph any card that folds unexpectedly when the top closes.
Board, print, inserts and the outside shipping pack remain separate specification decisions. A six-wall sample review gives fit and action observations, not a verified transport result or maximum load.
If the product is intentionally oriented to one wall, name that wall on the cavity plan. Then review the top opening from the same front position. A round item may look similar after rotation, while a card or attachment does not. The panel map should carry that difference so the artwork, holder and loading instruction use one orientation.

### Map the front and the back

The customer’s view shows only part of the arrangement. Earring posts, backs, clasps and chain storage can project behind the card. Record those parts in the mounted position and identify what they may touch. A card that sits flat by itself can require additional depth when the jewelry is attached.

Describe each attachment point by its job: holding an earring pair level, routing a chain, retaining a pendant or leaving a removal route. Ask for the proposed hole or slot geometry against the actual piece. An attractive pattern of cuts is not enough to identify how the item is loaded or removed.

Keep hanging or retail-fixture holes separate from jewelry attachment points. One supports the presented pack on a fixture; the other positions the jewelry. Combining their purposes without a layout can change the visible orientation and make the back of the card difficult to access.

Reserve clear space around each attachment for the assembly action. Front artwork and back copy should be reviewed with the jewelry mounted.

### Choose mounting rather than just information space

An information card carries instructions or brand copy. A mounting card adds an attachment and contact task, even when both are printed on similar material. A shaped box holder creates another set of loading and removal questions.

Decide whether the card travels in a pouch, rests in a box or hangs openly. That decision controls its outline, accessible edges and complete packed thickness. The jewelry measurement guide can help record posts, clasps and projections that the surrounding package must accommodate.

### Prepare an attachment layout brief

| Field | Record for the proposed card

| Jewelry | Piece count, geometry, weight, sensitive surfaces and mounted position.

| Attachment | Hole or slot locations, loading order, back fasteners and chain routing.

| Card | Finished outline, proposed material, folds and accessible removal edges.

| Surrounding pack | Pouch opening or box interior, complete thickness and packing sequence.

| Print | Front and back copy, cutout positions and protected artwork areas.

| Scope | Quantity by design, who mounts the jewelry and what the sample must answer.

For an illustrative necklace-and-earring set, the earrings may use front holes while the chain routes to the back. Show where the clasp sits and how the buyer releases the necklace. If a product variant changes those positions, review it as a mounted arrangement rather than substituting a photograph into the same artwork.

### Review the mounted piece, then its package

- Attach the actual jewelry using the proposed process.
- Inspect position, contact surfaces and the back of the card.
- Place the mounted card in its intended pouch or box.
- Open the complete pack and take out the card by hand.
- Remove the jewelry without making a new hole with the piece itself.
- Record changes to the cuts, copy, material or surrounding pack.

The original jewelry display-card guide demonstrates why jewelry attachment needs deliberate holes and routing. Use its method discussion for context; supplier-specific cutting dimensions and production instructions still need to match your actual design.

### Plan design quantities and mounting work

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. Record different card artworks and attachment layouts as design questions, with the proposed mounting work and packaging components identified. Final material, pricing, production conditions and sample scope are agreed for the project.

Get a Quote with front and back views of the mounted layout and the removal you want. Packrivo helps connect the card, jewelry and surrounding pack before the sample review.

### Request a Quote
Packrivo’s minimum is 500 pieces per design. Confirm the configuration, supplier proposal, sample scope and commercial terms for your project.Get a Quote

#### Structure

-

### Give the top cap its own interface
The lock-cap auto-bottom catalogue supports the named construction. Ask the converter to point to the cap tab and the matching receiver on the proposed blank. A lock name does not tell your packing team where to press, which panel moves first or how the customer releases it.
Show the carton both open and fully closed. On the open view, mark the final contents height, the cap sweep and any inside panel or slot receiving the lock. On the closed view, mark the grip used to release the cap. Keep a printed front label separate from those mechanical identities.

### Form the base before testing the cap
The auto-bottom carton focuses on prepared-base erection. The full-flap auto-bottom option adds a base coverage question. This lock-cap option adds upper engagement and customer release; record those as different actions instead of treating every automatic-bottom name as the same choice.
Have the packing owner form the bottom using the offered instructions and load the contents. Stop just before cap engagement and photograph the upper clearance. Then close and reopen using the intended grip. If a tab presses against the card or requires moving the product, locate that contact and ask for a drawing-based change. Do not hide the adjustment inside an informal packing habit.

### Keep a cap and base action table
For a hypothetical jewelry gift carton, the following record separates the empty setup from the loaded closing step.

| Action | Brief entry | Who confirms it
| Base erection | Prepared joint/base folds on drawing BX1 | Converter and packing owner
| Contents height | Jewelry holder plus the care card in its final position | Buyer supplies the complete set
| Cap engagement | Tab T1 enters receiver R1 on offered drawing | Converter identifies the mechanism
| Customer release | Named grip and opening movement | Buyer reviews loaded sample
| Card contact | Record any contact before changing the card position | Packing owner returns located evidence

### Make the closure decision reviewable
The converter’s next response should identify the tab, receiver and cap clearance, not simply confirm that a lock cap is available. The packing owner’s response should describe the sequence used with the actual contents. Your approval should reference both, with the sample and drawing revision named.
Read the general tuck-end carton option if a simple tuck closure is also being considered. Use the delivery-state record to keep factory preparation and local packing actions clear.

## Custom Paired Corrugated End Caps Sourcing
Paired corrugated end caps are internal supports placed around specified ends of a product or an inner pack. Consider this option when the proposed outer carton needs a defined relationship to those end areas. Record the product geometry, contact points and loading sequence so the two supports are designed and reviewed with the enclosure rather than chosen as separate pieces.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Keep the end-support geometry and outer-carton fit as one proposed configuration.

### Product specifications
Minimum orderMOQ 500 pieces per design.
Get a QuoteShare your dimensions, quantity and delivery destination.
The minimum is Packrivo’s project threshold. Availability for the chosen structure, material and finish is checked for your project.

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Name the ends that can be supported

Describe each product end independently. A handle, connector, lid or delicate edge might need clearance rather than contact. Show the regions the proposed support may touch and any region that should remain open. The relevant surface can be on the product itself or on an existing retail pack.

Ask how each end cap is formed and how it is held during loading. A folded corrugated arrangement can have several panels, locks or layers. The proposal should show the product-facing side and the side facing the outer carton so the two interfaces are clear.

The complete assembly determines the outer packing envelope. End supports can change the space between the inner product and the carton wall, and their positions may affect the room left for accessories. Record the assembled layout instead of measuring an empty carton first and asking the supports to fit whatever space remains.

### Compare end support with a complete inlay

A corrugated inlay option can describe a broader internal arrangement. Paired end caps focus the brief on two defined support interfaces. A divider separates multiple items and may not answer the same contact question. Compare the location, loading and removal task for each proposal.

For an illustrative boxed gift with an accessory, the end caps might locate the main inner pack while the accessory sits in a separate space. That space must remain part of the complete layout. It should not be assumed to support the accessory simply because the main item is held at both ends.

### Send the two interfaces in one brief

| Input | Record for the paired supports

| Product ends | Geometry, projections, complete weight and areas permitted to touch.

| End caps | Formed construction, folds, material proposal and retention.

| Outer carton | Assembled internal space and contact with each support.

| Accessories | Separate items, compartments and the complete loading order.

| Handling | Intended movement, route and evidence needed for the packed system.

| Project | Quantities, drawing versions, destination and sample questions.

Identify whether the two pieces are identical or handed left and right. That affects the component list and packing instructions. If one support changes, show which product or enclosure feature prompted the revision so the paired arrangement can be reviewed coherently.

Use visible identifiers in the working record when handed parts could be exchanged during loading.

### Review contact, loading and removal

- Form both proposed supports using the intended instructions.
- Place them on the named product or retail-pack ends.
- Load the assembly into the enclosure with all accessories.
- Inspect the support-to-product and support-to-carton interfaces.
- Close the outer pack, then review the intended opening and removal.
- Agree the transport review needed for that complete configuration.

Fit observations and shipping evidence answer different questions. Use the packed-system protection guide to keep the proposed supports, enclosure and route connected. A well-fitted static sample does not establish the result of a transport review.

### Agree the paired-component proposal

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. State the number of end-cap pieces per finished pack and identify any different designs. Final construction, material, pricing, sample work, production conditions and delivery timing are reviewed for the project.

The original corrugated end-cap reference illustrates end-positioned supports. It is a structural example, rather than evidence that this proposed Packrivo configuration has been tested.

Get a Quote with both product ends and the assembled layout. Packrivo helps organize the contact, component and sample questions before a proposal is accepted.

## Custom Paper Belly Band Sourcing
A paper belly band is a printed paper or board wrap placed around a product or an existing pack. Consider it when a gift needs a removable information or branding layer while the underlying box remains a separate component. Describe the wrapping path, supplied form and removal action so the band can be reviewed on the complete gift rather than as a flat graphic alone.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Connect wrap fit, seam and removal route to the complete contents.

### Product specifications

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Draw the actual wrapping path

Show which sides of the product or box the band passes over and where its joint lands. A wrap around a closed gift box may cross the lid opening, while a band around several loose components might hold them in a different arrangement. Name the intended task before deciding whether the band is a flat strip, an adhesive-ended piece or a prepared sleeve.

Measure the configuration in the state being wrapped. A bow, a folded tissue layer or another printed card can change the surface beneath the band. Mark the proposed fold positions and the areas that should remain visible. Asking for a tight wrap without these details leaves the removal action unresolved.

Locate the front artwork against the final joint and folds. If a seam belongs underneath the box, show that orientation in the brief. Keep any required product copy, barcode or care information readable after the band is applied.

### Compare the wrap with a label or structural sleeve

A label option introduces an attachment-to-surface task. A belly band can have a different application and removal sequence, depending on the proposed construction. Compare those actual steps rather than assuming every band slides off or every label leaves residue.

The sleeve around a drawer jewelry box is part of its opening structure. A separate belly band is an added wrap to specify in its own right. The names “band” and “packaging sleeve” can overlap, so the drawing and supplied form should decide the proposal.

### Brief the wrap as its own component

| Input | What to record

| Wrapped item | Complete dimensions, surface, added layers and orientation.

| Wrap path | Band width, folds, coverage and information that must remain visible.

| Joint | Location, proposed joining method and final appearance.

| Supply state | Flat strip, adhesive-ended part or prepared sleeve.

| Operation | Who applies it, how the customer opens it and what stays on the gift.

| Design plan | Artwork variants, quantities, underlying components and destination.

For an illustrative launch using one box structure and several band artworks, record which components remain identical and which change by design. The design quantity worksheet keeps those counts separate. A shared box does not make differently printed bands one design or establish a lower order basis.

### Review application, alignment and removal

- Place every layer on the gift in the proposed packing order.
- Apply the band using the intended supplied form and joining process.
- Inspect the joint, artwork face, fold locations and exposed information.
- Open or remove the band as the customer is expected to do.
- Inspect the underlying surface and any component the band touches.
- Record the reviewed wrap version and any affected box or print change.

If the proposed band slips or is difficult to remove, identify the wrapping dimension, construction or process that needs review. A color change will not answer a fit question. Resolve the application first, then evaluate a representative printed and finished version.

### Agree the complete scope

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. List the band and underlying box or product pack separately, including design quantities and sampling. Final materials, joining method, pricing, production conditions and delivery timing are confirmed for the project.

The original packaging-sleeve reference illustrates different supplied forms for this wrapping family. It provides structure context, without transferring the source’s commercial terms.

### Identify the flat variant rather than a carrier bag

Paper Mart’s flat merchandise bag page includes a No Gusset option and shows bags with an open serrated mouth and no carry handles. Its range also includes gusset variants, so keep the selected no-gusset form explicit in the brief.

Novolex’s Duro product line card places merchandise bags within its Pinch Bottom Bags group, separately from SOS and die-cut handle bags. These references establish a flat merchandise/pinch-bottom option; they do not supply the seam dimensions or usable cavity of your proposed bag.

Draw the two opposed faces, entry edge and closed lower edge. Ask the supplier to identify its longitudinal seam and bottom joint on that drawing. Do not invent a joint recipe from a front-view photograph.

### Review a flat pocket around the complete contents

Measure the sale-ready bundle with backing, tags and inner wrap. Keep its orientation during entry fixed. A thick addition or projecting tag can create a mouth or seam-contact question that outside width alone leaves open.

For a hypothetical gift-card bundle, mark a tied tag’s projecting point TP1 and the pocket mouth M1. Record where first contact occurs while the buyer uses the planned insertion grip. Do not rotate or compress the bundle without adding that action to the record.

The expandable parcel bag option reviews gusset expansion and seal reach around different fills. This flat pocket instead asks how one named merchandise envelope passes the mouth and sits against a closed bottom without a formed side gusset. It also differs from an SOS bag whose base is opened before loading.

### Use a mouth-to-bottom fit record

Copy these fields into the brief. The example locations help the supplier find an observation; they are not specified tolerances.

| Location or state | Input to identify | Observation to keep

| Flat form | No side gusset; two faces and bottom-joint drawing | Received flat configuration matches the selected variant

| Mouth M1 | Usable opening and full bundle entry orientation | First contact at tag TP1 or another named point

| Longitudinal seam | Supplier-marked seam location | Bundle edge beside it during entry and removal

| Pinch bottom B1 | Actual closed-bottom construction and usable depth | Lower bundle edge in the loaded pocket

| Top treatment | Left open or separately proposed closing action | Space and work remaining above the merchandise

| Customer removal | Grip and exit route through M1 | Changed hand action or contact requiring review

### Inspect the delivered pocket before adding artwork

Confirm whether the bottom and longitudinal seam arrive completed. Receive the sample in the proposed material, then inspect the mouth, joint locations and flat state before loading it. Identify any substitute model separately.

Insert the complete bundle, locate its lower edge against B1 and review removal through M1. If the supplier changes the mouth or joint position, repeat those same actions. The front artwork can remain unchanged while the usable pocket changes.

If a carry grip is required, compare the gift carrier and tissue set. A flat pocket’s fit record does not establish carrying capacity, a barrier or distribution performance.

### Map the side tab to its receiving slot

### Choose rectangular, square or circular roll labels by the application

| Shape | Dimensions and artwork to supply | Check on the applied candidate

| Rectangle | Finished width and height, corner shape, artwork orientation and the available panel. | Readable content and edge placement on the actual flat or curved surface.

## Custom Regular Slotted Shipping Carton Sourcing
A regular slotted shipping carton uses a joined body with top and bottom flaps that are closed after forming and loading. Consider this outer-pack option when a retail gift, several small boxes or a coordinated set needs a defined shipping unit. Start with the complete inner arrangement and closure process so the carton proposal covers what is actually packed and sent.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Size the outward carton around the complete inner pack and its closing method.

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Describe the shipping unit, not the retail box

Record every item going into the carton. Individual gift boxes, separators, protective layers and printed extras all occupy space and affect the loading sequence. Show their arrangement before asking for an internal carton size. A retail box’s external dimensions are one input, rather than the final shipping dimensions.

The top and bottom flap arrangement introduces a sealing task. Ask for the proposed body joint, flap layout and closing method. Identify which actions happen before loading and how the packing team checks that the base is ready. The shipping label needs a defined outside surface after the pack is sealed.

State the route and handling context to be reviewed. A parcel containing one gift and a master carton carrying several retail units are different packed systems. A construction name helps identify the proposed carton, while material selection and transport review still depend on the complete configuration.

### Compare outer-carton and mailer tasks

A front-tuck mailer gives a different flap and opening sequence. A regular slotted carton commonly brings separate top and bottom sealing into the packing work. Compare the loaded arrangement, supplies and customer or receiving-team opening process rather than choosing solely by the appearance of the empty shell.

For an illustrative group of candle gift boxes, the retail boxes might already include fitted holders. That does not decide the outer-carton arrangement. Show the number and orientation of retail units, the proposed separation between them and the materials used around the group. The review should refer to that complete pack.

### Prepare a shipping-carton specification

| Field | Describe the actual shipping unit

| Contents | Retail units, accessories, counts, dimensions and complete weight.

| Layout | Loading orientation, dividers, surrounding materials and stacking within the carton.

| Construction | Proposed body joint, flap arrangement, material and closing method.

| Operations | Base preparation, loading, sealing, labeling and receiving opening.

| Route | Pickup and destination, parcel or grouped shipment and review requirements.

| Records | Carton version, artwork, design quantities and sample scope.

Keep net contents, packing materials and complete carton weight distinct in the record. Use the carton and freight data worksheet once the proposed shipping unit is clear. Identify estimates so a freight inquiry does not treat an early layout as measured shipment data.

### Review forming, sealing and the packed system

- Form the carton and close the base using the intended method.
- Load the complete inner arrangement in its specified order.
- Inspect clearances, dividers, contact and remaining space.
- Close and seal the top, then place the planned shipping information.
- Agree the appropriate transport review with the relevant responsible party.
- Record the configuration and evidence used for the acceptance decision.

Separate fit observations from transport evidence. A pack that closes comfortably has answered an assembly question; a different review is needed for the intended shipment. If a carton size or material changes, identify which loading, sealing or transport questions must be checked again.

### Agree the carton and packing scope

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. List the outer carton, dividers, sealing supplies and any packing work separately. Final material, pricing, production conditions, sample scope and delivery timing are reviewed for the project.

The original box-style reference explains the regular slotted container’s flap arrangement. The FEFCO construction reference supports identifying corrugated designs by drawing. These references describe construction, rather than certifying this proposed shipment.

This is a packaging concept. The actual material, opening feature and both closure stages must be identified in the supplier proposal.

### Request the complete return sequence

PaperPak’s eCommerce mailer range describes a zipper-style tear strip and two adhesive strips, with one used for the outward closure and another for return use. Its Everyday Mailer flyer confirms those features. That is a specific structural reference, not a statement that every returnable paper bag uses them.
For a dual-strip proposal, ask for a drawing identifying the first strip, tear path, second strip and each receiving surface. Confirm their positions and the customer sequence on the actual project sample.

State whether the same contents are returned or a different packed configuration is expected. Include all inner wrap and backing. The return job can create a different filled envelope from the outward parcel.

Compare the paper mailer brief when only one closing stage is required. Here, keep the second stage as an explicit construction and user-action question.

### Review what survives first opening

Ask how the outward label and return label are handled. A second closure does not by itself solve a conflicting label or an obscured address. Keep those surface and instruction decisions separate.

### Use a return-stage record

Keep all states on the same sample identity. A fresh second bag should not stand in for the used return stage.

| Stage | Closure construction in the supplier drawing | Evidence
| First closing | First adhesive strip and its receiving surface | Loaded outward state
| First opening | Located tear path and customer action | Used bag immediately afterwards
| Return loading | Complete returned contents | Filled used-bag view
| Second closing | Second strip, remaining liner and receiving surface | Same bag before and after closing
| Label change | Outward and return surface plan | Address visibility in return state

Keep the second strip unused during outward packing and first opening. Check its position and the remaining receiving surface immediately after the tear action. If the proposed mechanism differs from the reference, identify it as a separate configuration rather than assuming this dual-strip instruction applies.

### Make the user instruction specific

In the hypothetical record, name the first opening O1 and second closure S2. Ask the supplier what O1 must preserve for S2. If the intended opening damages that area, the sequence needs a revised proposal.

Keep the supplied state, packing responsibility and any protective inner pack attached to the brief. Changes to the opening feature can reopen the return instruction even if the outside artwork stays unchanged.

Any repeated-use, sealing or distribution requirement needs a separately agreed evaluation. This record concerns one proposed outward-and-return sequence without promising reusable performance across unspecified conditions.

## Custom Returnable Poly Parcel Bag Sourcing
A returnable poly mailer needs the outbound seal, opening zone and unused return seal identified separately. Review them around the actual primary pack, then record the return-label area. The film seam and liner arrangement are specific procurement questions that should be visible alongside the two-journey packing sequence.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Keep the first opening route clear of the intended return-seal feature.

### Product specifications
Minimum orderMOQ 500 pieces per design.
Get a QuoteShare your dimensions, quantity and delivery destination.
The minimum is Packrivo’s project threshold. Availability for the chosen structure, material and finish is checked for your project.

### What affects the price

#### Size

-

#### Structure

-

#### Materials

#### Printing & finishes
