#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

This concept illustrates a proposed return arrangement. The offered film, seams and seal construction are confirmed for the project.

### Map the two seals before selecting the bag size
The original poly-mailer construction brochure identifies a second adhesive strip for return mailing and tear/perforation options. Ask for the actual offered layout showing both adhesive strips, their release liners and the opening zone between the intended stages.
Provide the complete primary-pack envelope, including rigid corners and any inner protective arrangement. Record how the bag is filled, where its film seams meet those corners and which part of the lip folds over to make the first seal. Keep these dimensions separate from the nominal flat bag size.
Identify the film/adhesive/seam proposal by grade or drawing reference. A returnable name does not specify the weld, liner or label surface. The converter should explain those fields for the configuration being offered.

### Review opening without consuming the return closure
Load the proposed bag, apply the first seal using the agreed packing method and open it through the offered route. Inspect the location of the untouched return strip and its liner. Record whether the opening action removes or obstructs a feature needed for the next closure.
Reload the complete primary pack and use the return strip through the intended action. Add the proposed return-label arrangement. If the previous label must be covered or replaced, record the owner and method as part of the return instructions. Keep the label readable area clear of the closing movement.
Compare the returnable paper parcel bag with the same primary pack. This film configuration adds welded seam, liner and opening-zone questions. The two materials should be compared through their offered constructions and actual handling evidence, without assuming a transit or reuse rating.

### Use a two-stage seal record
The following hypothetical boxed-gift return plan names operations without claiming completed journeys.

| Stage or interface | Input to mark | Evidence for review
| Outbound strip S1 | First seal and liner L1 on drawing | Loaded lip-fold view
| Opening zone O1 | Offered tear or opening route | Opened bag with S2 still identified
| Return strip S2 | Unused liner L2 and resealing action | Reloaded and resealed sample
| Film seams | Primary-box corner relationships | Located seam/contact observations
| Return label | Readable surface and old-label action | Packed label/closure map

### Keep the customer instructions with the sample
The converter should return the two-strip layout and film/seam identities. The packing owner should document the first seal and customer opening route used during review. The buyer should decide whether those instructions leave a clear return action for the actual pack. Record any further distribution evaluation separately for the intended complete system.
Use the existing mailer options to compare a rigid outer enclosure if that is also under consideration. Bring the primary-pack envelope, seal map and return-label question to Get a Quote. MOQ is 500 pieces per design; materials, sampling and commercial details are confirmed for the project.

## Custom Reverse Tuck Retail Carton Sourcing
Choose a reverse tuck carton when the two ends need opposite tuck directions, then label the packing end and customer opening end on the drawing. Review the loaded carton from both sides before approving artwork. The structure name alone doesn't settle which end staff should close first.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Name the loading and customer ends against the same front reference.

### Product specifications
Minimum orderMOQ 500 pieces per design.
Get a QuoteShare your dimensions, quantity and delivery destination.
The minimum is Packrivo’s project threshold. Availability for the chosen structure, material and finish is checked for your project.

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

This is a packaging option for an inquiry. The board, dimensions, closure detail and delivery state need a project proposal and sample review. Start with the product in its sale condition, including any card, wrap or holder that travels inside the carton.

### Make the opposite ends visible on the drawing
PakFactory’s reverse tuck reference identifies a reversed bottom end compared with the straight tuck style. Use that narrow structural distinction to discuss flap orientation; it doesn’t establish the strength of your finished pack.
Mark the front panel with a plain arrow on your working drawing. Next, mark the hinges of the top and bottom tuck flaps. Keep those marks in a structural layer so the artwork team can see which panel each end folds towards.
A long product may enter from one end yet need removal from the other. Identify that intention explicitly. If either end may be opened in retail use, review both actions rather than treating the bottom as invisible.

### Keep the loading route separate from the printed front
A hypothetical jewelry card has a hanging hole at one end and a thicker ornament at the other. The card can be placed into the carton with its ornament leading or trailing. Those two loading routes deserve separate trials because the card and end flaps meet in different places.
Lay the loaded carton down in the intended sales orientation. Open the customer end without rotating the pack to make the action easier. Observe where fingers enter, whether the product catches on a flap, and which printed panel is visible during removal.
Compare this with the existing tuck-end gift box option when you only need a general carton brief. The extra decision here is the relationship between two opposed end closures and two different users.

### Use an end-by-end configuration record
Fill this record against a numbered drawing revision. An empty cell means the decision remains open; it should stay visible in the inquiry.

| End | Action to agree | Sample evidence
| Packing end | Entry face and leading product edge | Photo before the last flap closes
| Customer end | Opening face and grip route | Photo with the product partly removed
| Opposite end | Tuck direction and flap overlap | Closed end and reopened end
| Artwork | Front face, back face and reading direction | Loaded pack standing in sales position
Review board grade and caliper independently of this end map. If a proposed insert changes the product’s height or orientation, repeat the removal check with that insert in place.

### Agree what arrives at the packing bench
Request a photo or drawing of the carton as delivered, not only as assembled. State whether a side seam is prepared, which end remains open, and who forms each closure. Storage and packing plans should use that supplied state.
On the sample, close each end in the proposed sequence, check the print direction, and remove the product through the intended opening. Record a visible obstruction as a location and action rather than a general comment such as ‘too tight’. These are fit observations, not a transport test or a load rating.
Keep a separate end-map row if the same carton proposal is being discussed for a second product card. Identify the card’s leading edge and removal face again. Sharing a printed design doesn’t make two loading routes identical. The comparison should show which product configuration was actually reviewed through each end.

### Send the two routes with your inquiry
Send product dimensions, the complete packed contents, your end map, destination and quantity through Get a Quote. Packrivo’s minimum is 500 pieces per design. Materials, sample scope, price and timing are confirmed for the project after the brief and supplier questions are reviewed.

### Locate the joint that makes the cell fixed
Sonoco’s partition construction reference describes fold-over glue wall strips with opposite strips perforated, folded and glued. Use that description to ask for the offered strip and joint map. It supports a construction choice; it does not establish the performance of your particular gift set.
Label the long and short strips, their intersections and the folded attachment faces. ### Compare fixed joints with a loose grid
The paperboard grid divider creates a separate slot/intersection task. Compare its assembly and removal route with the proposed fold-over glued joint. Keep the item arrangement constant so the comparison explains who prepares the partition and what remains in place during packing.
The existing corrugated divider option is another compartment approach. Record the supplied state and box relationship rather than treating board names as proof that one partition is suitable.
Review the offered partition empty, fully loaded and partly empty after an item is removed. Inspect the actual joint and box seating in each state. If a crossing strip moves with the product, locate the movement before asking for a change. Record that observation without assuming a universal retention result.

## Custom Rigid Paperboard Postal Envelope Sourcing
A rigid paperboard postal envelope encloses a flat item through a defined opening and closing flap. Consider this option for a small printed gift or another flat configuration when the proposed panel construction and opening process suit the contents. Record the complete packed thickness, corners and customer access so the envelope can be assessed as a finished parcel, rather than as an empty flat shape.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Review the complete thickness, loading mouth and customer removal route.

### Product specifications
Minimum orderMOQ 500 pieces per design.
Get a QuoteShare your dimensions, quantity and delivery destination.
The minimum is Packrivo’s project threshold. Availability for the chosen structure, material and finish is checked for your project.

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Describe the complete flat configuration

List the item, its cover and every additional layer. A greeting card, print bundle or presentation backing can change both thickness and corner contact. Record the contents in the state they will enter the envelope, including any uneven or projecting areas.

Ask for the proposed front and back panel construction, folds and opening geometry. The term “rigid” is a packaging option here, not a measured bending result. If the product has a specific requirement for flatness or handling, identify it for the relevant supplier and agree how it will be reviewed.

Locate the closing flap after the envelope is loaded. A thick item can change where the flap reaches and how the sealing surface contacts it. Describe the intended sealing and customer-opening method, with any tear feature or adhesive component included in the proposed material list.

Mark the outside label orientation on the closed view so shipping information stays clear of the opening feature and closing joint.

### Compare an envelope with a wrapping pack

A paper mailer bag brings a different panel and filled-shape discussion. A formed mailer box adds a box cavity and closing-flap sequence. Compare these alternatives against the product’s thickness, corners, loading and opening, rather than assuming every flat gift can use the same outer pack.

For an illustrative print-and-card gift, the small card needs a defined position so it does not enter the flap or customer opening path. A backing sheet might be proposed to support the print, but it should be listed as a separate component with its own material and contact questions.

### Prepare a thickness and access brief

| Field | Describe the packed envelope

| Contents | Complete length, width, thickness, weight and sensitive corners.

| Layers | Covers, backing, small extras and their positions.

| Envelope | Panel construction, usable opening, folds and seam layout.

| Closure | Flap reach, proposed seal and customer access route.

| Information | Print faces, address area and shipping-label surface.

| Project | Design quantities, destination, route and sample decisions.

Specify whether a single configuration or several thicknesses must be reviewed. The measurement method worksheet helps define the assembled condition used for comparison. Keep the empty envelope dimensions separate from those of the sealed parcel.

### Review loading, closure and receipt

- Prepare the complete product and its additional layers.
- Load it through the intended opening without forcing corners or edges.
- Inspect the panel shape and flap position with the contents inside.
- Apply the proposed seal and shipping information.
- Open the parcel and remove the contents as the customer would.
- Agree the handling or transport evidence needed for the intended route.

A supplied sample can reveal a tight opening or a flap that reaches the wrong position. Describe that observation against the configuration reviewed. Changes to thickness, panel material or the seal may require the affected access and route questions to be checked again.

### Plan the project without assumed postage

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. Identify the envelope, backing, printed extras and sampling separately. Carrier classification, charges and route suitability depend on the actual shipment; final materials, production conditions, pricing and delivery timing are agreed for the project.

The board-mailer catalogue examples show envelope-like formats with several closure and loading options. Use them as format context; agree the material, packed thickness, closure and carrier classification for the actual shipment.

Get a Quote with the packed thickness and receipt experience. Packrivo helps connect those details to a supplier proposal and a clear sample review.

### Place the product on the proposed wrap panel

Record the supplied state, any prepared seam and who completes the lock. Ask how the folded pack is intended to be handled and whether another outer pack is part of the project. Fit observations should remain separate from any required distribution evaluation.

| Reference | Pouch/document/pack revisions | Matched component record

Keep a missing adhesion or view requirement open. A supplier’s generic document-wallet name doesn’t resolve the proposed surface or data location.

## Custom Shoulder-Neck Rigid Box Sourcing

## Custom Shoulder-Neck Rigid Box Sourcing
A shoulder and neck rigid box uses an inner board section around the base to guide the lid and define the visible opening. Consider this option when a jewelry or candle gift needs a lift-off reveal with a deliberate display height. Brief the base, inner neck, lid and product holder together so an attractive closed box also opens comfortably around the actual contents.
AI-assisted concept illustration. Review the neck, holder and closed-lid relationship with the actual contents.

### Product specifications
Minimum orderMOQ 500 pieces per design.
Get a QuoteShare your dimensions, quantity and delivery destination.
The minimum is Packrivo’s project threshold. Availability for the chosen structure, material and finish is checked for your project.

### What affects the price
-

#### Size
The finished dimensions and usable interior determine material use and product fit.
-

#### Structure
Closures, folds and assembly steps change the work needed to make the pack.
-

#### Materials
Board, wrap and insert choices affect both construction and material cost.
-

#### Printing & finishes
Artwork coverage, colors and decoration steps change setup and finishing costs.
-

#### Quantity
The quantity per design changes how setup and production costs are distributed.

Concept / packaging option. AI-assisted visuals explain the structure; final materials, production details and sample scope are agreed for the project.

### Locate the lid against the neck

The inner neck introduces a second reference inside the outer shell. Its exposed height, the lid depth and the position of the contents all affect what the customer sees after lifting the lid. Ask for a section drawing showing which surface stops the lid, where the lid overlaps the neck and how the base supports the product. A view of the exterior alone leaves those relationships unclear.

Describe whether the closed lid should cover the neck completely or leave part visible. Then identify the product’s highest point, including clasps, lids, bows or a folded care card. Keep that height separate from the box’s exterior height. A holder that raises the product can change lid clearance even when the outer dimensions stay the same.

Include the inside wrapping and seams in the drawing discussion. Paper, lining and folded edges occupy space at the lid interface. Their proposed construction belongs in the fit review before the final artwork is positioned on the shell.

### Choose the opening around the product

Compare this structure with a simple lid-and-base jewelry box when you mainly need a separate cover. The shoulder and neck adds an internal interface to specify. A hinged format creates a different opening path and needs room behind the lid; a drawer requires room in front for the tray to travel.

Think through the first action after the lid is removed. Can the customer lift a candle vessel directly, reach a jewelry pull point or remove an entire holder? A close-fitting neck may affect finger access. Describe the intended movement, rather than asking for a box that simply looks tight. For a set, identify which piece is removed first and where the remaining pieces stay.

### Build a section-based brief

| Field | What to record

| Contents | Each item, weight, display orientation and projecting parts.

| Base and neck | Usable interior, neck position, exposed height and contact surfaces.

| Lid | Depth, overlap, stopping surface and preferred removal movement.

| Holder | Support height, cavity layout, removal point and attachment method.

| Presentation | Inside and outside artwork, wrapping seams and intended finish areas.

| Project | Quantity by design, destination, packing state and the decisions still open.

Use the dimension and acceptance method worksheet to name each critical feature. Have the responsible parties agree how the neck, lid and product space will be measured. Proposed dimensions can start the discussion, but they should remain identified as estimates until reviewed.

### Review the sample as a sequence

- Place the complete product and proposed holder in the base.
- Lower and lift the lid without using the product to push it into place.
- Inspect alignment, neck exposure, stopping position and the customer grip.
- Remove the product in the intended sequence and examine contact areas.
- Repeat the opening, then record the version and any change requested.

For an illustrative necklace set, a care card placed above the jewelry could change the lid decision. Reviewing the empty shell would miss that interaction. Keep the card, holder and jewelry in the configuration used for approval, and describe a correction by its affected feature rather than saying the box feels wrong.

### Plan quantity and the next decision

Packrivo’s order planning policy is 500 pieces per product / design. Separate the shell, holder and any additional components in the proposal, with their quantities and sample work identified. Project materials, pricing, production conditions and delivery timing are confirmed against the agreed specification.

The manufacturer’s shoulder and neck reference illustrates the additional internal board interface. It supports the structural distinction, rather than a Packrivo performance or supply claim.

Get a Quote with the product layout and the opening you want. Packrivo can help organize the section questions, compare the replies and define what the next sample needs to resolve.

| Stop | Part to inspect | Decision

| Conversion | Panel being moved or removed | Staff action without disturbing units

If the closed carton is intended to travel without another outer pack, ask for an appropriate distribution evaluation proposal. A successful display conversion doesn’t establish transit performance. Keep the review scope and any agreed tests written separately.
If the upper panel becomes a header behind the units, check the face that ends up visible after conversion. Record whether the printed information reads upright and whether the panel meets another part. The conversion record should connect that final header view to the same action staff will perform, rather than to a separate staged display image.
